# CLAUDE.md — 识字 Zeichentrainer

Working language: **English.** Reply to H in English. Short, direct, no excessive politeness.

## What this is
Chinese character trainer for adults (spaced repetition), a reinterpretation of 悟空识字 without the kids' aesthetic.
User: H, product manager, Beijing, **phone-only (Android/Xiaomi, Chrome, VPN), no computer**.
UI language: English. Learning content: Chinese + pinyin + English meaning.

## Live deployment
- Repo: `henglicam/zeichentrainer` · GitHub Pages, branch `main`, folder `/ (root)`.
- URL: `https://henglicam.github.io/zeichentrainer/`
- Push to `main` → Pages rebuilds automatically (~1–2 min). `index.html` must stay in the repo root.
- The site is **public** (free plan). User data lives exclusively on the device (IndexedDB), never in the repo; the only thing the app sends on its own is the daily anonymous usage row (v170, below), and that can be switched off.

## Current state (PWA v389, 2026-09-09)
- **Files:** `index.html` · `styles.css` · `lang.js` (the app's texts in other languages, v253) · `app.js` · `manifest.webmanifest` (with the `share_target` for screenshots) · `sw.js` · `privacy.html` (the Play Store's privacy page, not in the shell; since v170 it also describes the daily usage counts, since v173 the framed area to the AI) · `signs.json` (phrasebook) · `nmt-model.json` (translation-model manifest) · `icon-192/512/maskable-512.png` · `vendor/` (Tesseract + the simplified and traditional readers + dictionaries + OpenCC's character tables both ways + the stroke medians of 9,534 characters `strokes.txt.gz` 2.4 MB, ~16 MB, `vendor/nmt/` Bergamot engine 5 MB + zh→en model 50 MB, licenses in `vendor/LICENSES.txt`) · `.github/workflows/fetch-nmt-model.yml` · `.github/workflows/purge-mirror.yml` (v327) · `SPEC-sign-cards.md` · `README.md` (German, the one-time hosting steps for the phone — not a feature description). Vanilla JS, no frameworks, no build step. `app.js` in reading order: helpers and state → IndexedDB → online AI → study screen → cards, detail, edit form → More → camera and inbox → reading pipeline (`readPass`, `readPassTra`/`traWorker`/`t2s`, `secondLook`, `readingScore`, `deskewBlob`, `textRowExtent`, `toBW`/`chromaCanvas`/`toChroma`/`inkHeight`) → character picker and drawing sheet → sign editor and save → service worker, mirror, shell check and the shared screenshots (`takeShared`). The usage counters and the owner's gate sit with More. Small shared helpers: `askSheet` (a question as the app's own sheet, v222), `putCard`, `pySpaced`, `eachLine`, `slineHTML`/`wireSlines`, `urlOf`, `fullPhoto`, `pageVersion`, `median`, `readingStatus`, `logErr`/`diagText`/`shareDiag`, `busyHTML`, `apiErrText` (the error text from a provider's or the relay's JSON body).
- **PWA:** installed and verified on the Xiaomi; runs offline (SW cache-first); `navigator.storage.persist()` granted. **Versioning:** SW cache `zt-vN` in `sw.js` and the label `PWA vN` in `index.html` (`.ver` — **hidden since v225**, H's "Go" on the design review: a developer's line, not a user's; it stays in the page because the shell check, the mirror update and About read it, and More → About shows "PWA vN." to the user) move in lockstep — bump both on **every** change to cached files. The app updates itself (update check on load/foreground, auto-reload on `controllerchange` — deferred to the next foreground while the app is in use since v279); the worker's install fetches the shell with `cache:"reload"` — GitHub Pages sends `max-age=600`, and after two deploys within ten minutes a new worker once filled its cache with the previous shell (the phone showed the v70 page with the v69 script: header v70, no drag in the sheet). **Boot self-check** (`shellCheck`): `APP_V` in `app.js` must equal the `PWA vN` label — **bump both, plus `zt-vN`, on every change**; if they differ, the page posts `refresh` to the worker, which refills its cache from the server (`cache:"reload"`) and the page reloads once (through `reloadSoon` since v279, see below; guard: at most once per 10 min, setting `shellFixAt`); it runs after registration and on the first `controllerchange`; the vendor cache `zt-ocr-v1` survives shell updates — bump it only when vendor files change.
- **The reload after an update waits until the app is idle (v279, H after eight builds in two hours: "nach Laden eines neuen Builds flackert die App … Passiert das nach jedem Update auch bei den Usern?" → the answer: one reload per update, two with a mixed shell; the flicker was the pace of the deploys → "Go" on the deferral):** until v278 the page reloaded the moment the new worker took over, in the middle of whatever H was doing. Now `reloadSoon()` decides: within `RELOAD_GRACE` 3 s of the load (the app was just opened, the first screen is up) or while the app is in the background the page reloads at once, as before; later `RELOAD_DUE` is set and the reload comes when the app next comes to the foreground (`visibilitychange`). The same for the shell check's "refreshed" reload and the hourly mirror check's "updated"; a tap on the mirror row's **Check now** still reloads at once (`MIRROR.forced`) — the user asked for it. The old page keeps running meanwhile with the new worker's cache, which is harmless (the vendor cache is separate, the page's own files are already loaded). Suite `verify-reload` (a synthetic `controllerchange` in the first seconds reloads at once, one after the grace leaves the page with `RELOAD_DUE`, a hide changes nothing, the return to the foreground reloads, an update while hidden reloads at once); the shell and english suites pass. **The mirror suite fails in this session on both v278 and v279** with "The mirror is not reachable: Failed to fetch" from the worker's fetch of the suite's fake mirror — a sandbox or harness problem, not the app (the shell suite, which uses the same reload path, passes). Not yet field-checked — the next update after v279 is the check: H should see no reload while using the app, and the new version on the next return to the foreground or the next open. **Never while a photo is on its way from the camera (v316, H right after the v315 update: "Ich habe gerade ein Foto gemacht und bin danach direkt auf der Learn Seite gelandet. Foto ist weg. Warum?" — Diagnostics at v315 with the previous reading's log restored from the setting and no line for the new photo):** the camera app in front is the page hidden, the new worker took over meanwhile, `reloadSoon` reloaded the hidden page at once, and the camera handed its photo to a page that was gone — the file input's change never reached the app, the photo never reached the inbox. (The v279 rule also reloads on the return to the foreground, which comes before the change event; the same loss.) Now `PICKING` is set by the tap on Take photo or From album and cleared by the input's `change` or `cancel` (at most `PICK_MAX` ten minutes), and `reloadBusy()` = `picking()` or a frame standing (`CROP`, the photo open in crop mode, Crop again too): `reloadSoon` only sets `RELOAD_DUE` then, hidden or not, and the return to the foreground reloads only when neither holds — so the reload comes with the next foreground after the photo is saved or cancelled. Suite `verify-reload` (Take photo tapped arms the flag; headless Chromium then fires the input's `cancel` at once, since no dialog opens, so the case arms the flag again by hand — a worker change while hidden: no reload, `RELOAD_DUE` set; the return to the foreground: no reload; the photo landing in crop mode: a hide and return still no reload; the frame gone: the next return reloads); the english suite passes. Not yet field-checked — the next update taken through the camera is the check. What it cannot do: bring the lost photo back, and a page Android discards while the camera runs (memory) reloads all the same and loses the photo as well — that is the system's, not the update's. **The reload comes at the next pause, on the same screen, and the mirror is fresh within minutes (v327, H: "make sure that new software versions always load automatically without the need for manual refresh"):** three faults kept an update from showing by itself. A reload deferred past the 3 s grace waited for the next return to the foreground, so an update that arrived while H used the app (the worker's install takes a few seconds, longer than the grace) showed only after leaving and reopening the app. The mirror was asked at most once an hour. And jsDelivr keeps its own copy of a branch's files for hours, so a phone without a VPN saw a new version late even when it asked. Now: **(1) the idle reload** — once a reload is due, every `RELOAD_POLL` 2 s the page asks `reloadIdle()`: no finger for `IDLE_MS` 4 s (`LAST_TOUCH` from pointerdown, keydown, input, touchstart, wheel), the page visible, no photo on its way and no frame (`reloadBusy`, v316), no Edit or Add form, no reading waiting (`PENDING`, `READING`), no Translate-all run, no sheet or drawing sheet open, nothing typed in the Feedback box — and then `reloadNow()` notes the screen in setting `resumeView` (mode, the open card, the Cards search, the scroll) and reloads; `boot` puts the screen back (within `RESUME_MAX` 3 min; a card that is gone falls back to the list) and drops the setting, so the reload is a flicker on the same screen. The rules of v279 and v316 stay: within the grace or in the background at once, never while a photo is picked or a frame stands; the return to the foreground reloads as before; the mirror row's Check now reloads at once through `reloadNow` too. **(2) The mirror every ten minutes** (`MIRROR_EVERY`, on start, on foreground and on a timer while the app is open; hourly until v326), and `reg.update()` on the same timer. **(3) The mirror's cache purged on every push to main** — `.github/workflows/purge-mirror.yml` calls `purge.jsdelivr.net` for the shell files (index, app, styles, lang, sw, manifest, signs, nmt-model, privacy); the purge API needs no key; the sandbox cannot reach jsDelivr (403 through the proxy), so whether the purge answers is seen in the repo's Actions tab — **not yet seen**; if it fails, the mirror lags by jsDelivr's own cache and nothing else changes. What it costs: a reload while H reads a card front without touching it for four seconds — the same card comes back, the session's queue is rebuilt from the deck (a card marked "again" earlier in the session may come later than it would have); a mirror fetch of index.html every ten minutes while the app is open. Suite `verify-reload` (the early reload; a due reload held while the finger taps every second on More, then coming within 4 s of the last touch with More restored at the same scroll and the setting gone; the Cards detail with the search restored; the Add form holding the reload with the finger at rest and the reload after leaving it; an update in the background at once; the v316 pick cases, the standing frame holding through 5 s at rest); the english, shell and autocard suites pass. Not yet field-checked — the next update is the check: it should appear within seconds of a pause, on the screen H was on.
- **No VPN needed to use the app (H's requirement):** the installed app runs from its cache, DeepSeek is reachable from China, and updates also arrive through a **mirror** — More → Updates without a VPN (setting `mirror`, default jsDelivr `cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/henglicam/zeichentrainer@main/`, **field-verified reachable from H's phone without VPN**). The page posts `mirror-update` (with its own version label) to the worker on start/foreground (at most hourly) or on "Check now"; the worker reads `PWA vN` from the mirror's index.html and, if newer, fetches the shell assets into the current cache — content types by extension, because jsDelivr hands HTML out as text/plain — and the page reloads (`mirrorUpdate` in `sw.js`, `mirrorCheck` in `app.js`). **Vendor files come from the mirror too** (H: "text recognition does not work without VPN" — the reader's ~12 MB are fetched on first use, and github.io was unreachable): the worker tries the origin, and on failure or a non-OK answer fetches `MIRROR + vendor/…` (all OCR/dictionary files are under jsDelivr's 20 MB cap; types by extension), caching the result in `zt-ocr-v1`; the page tells the worker the mirror setting on start (`tellMirror`). **A silent origin counts as a failure** (v189, H's phone without the VPN: "Reading the text … still at: loading the reader …" for good — github.io behind the wall answers with nothing, and the fallback waited for an error that never came): a vendor fetch from the origin gets `ORIGIN_WAIT` 6 s, then the mirror takes over, and after one such failure the mirror is asked first for `ORIGIN_DOWN` five minutes (`withTimeout`, `originDownUntil` in `sw.js`); measured: the first file 6 s, the rest milliseconds, the whole reader download 6.6 s against a silent origin. **A page no worker controls reads through the mirror all the same, and asks the worker to take it over (v335, H, 2026-09-08, the Xiaomi without the VPN: "Now he really loads forever!" — Diagnostics `SW no`, the reading at "loading the reader …" for minutes, and `Uncaught Error: TypeError: Failed to fetch @tesseract.min.js:2`):** the v189 rule is the worker's, and without a controlling worker every reader file went straight to github.io, which answers with nothing behind the wall; Tesseract's own fetch of the core or the language file then never settled, and nothing ended the reading. Why the worker had stopped controlling the page is not known (earlier diagnostics the same day said `SW yes` with the same user agent; the registration is not dropped by a failed update, and `sw.js` never unregisters) — Diagnostics say more since: `SW no (registration active)` / `(registration failed: …)` / `(registration none)` (`SW_REG`) and `· reader files from the mirror` when the page chose it. Three things now. **(1) The claim:** once `navigator.serviceWorker.ready` resolves and the page has no controller, the page posts `claim` and the worker calls `clients.claim()` — the page is controlled at once, no reload (the first-controller branch of `controllerchange` runs `tellMirror` and the shell check); a worker that is active but not controlling — a hard reload, a worker that activated while the page was open without claiming — is the state this fixes; a page with no worker at all (a first install behind the wall, a failed registration) gets (2). **(2) The page's own origin-then-mirror rule** (`vendorBase`, `vendorFetch`): with no controller the page asks github.io once per session for `vendor/t2s.txt` with `cache:"no-store"` and `ORIGIN_WAIT` 6 s; silent → the mirror (`mirrorURL()+"vendor/"`) for every reader file, the note "no worker controls the page and github.io did not answer within 6 s — the reader's files come from the mirror" in the error log (so the daily row and All users carry it, v271) and in Diagnostics' head line. Every file the page loads itself goes through `vendorFetch` — the scripts (`loadScript` fetches `./vendor/…` and runs it from a blob URL, with or without the worker), the dictionary, the character tables, the strokes, the Text recognition download —, from the reader's cache `zt-ocr-v1` first when the page has no worker (`caches.match`), and a file fetched past the worker is put into that cache under its origin address, so the worker serves it later and a reload without the worker reads it from the phone. Tesseract's worker and core come as blob URLs from `vendorFetch` (`tessPaths`; the core's URL carries `#core.js`, since the worker only checks that the path ends in "js" — measured: the `.wasm.js` is a single-file build, no separate `.wasm` is fetched), the language file Tesseract fetches itself from the base the probe chose — this build's `initialize` step cannot take the data directly (it joins `data` instead of `code` into the language name; `{code,data}` was tried and failed with "Failed loading language '31,139,…'"), so without the worker 1.7 MB come from the network on every start. **(3) A stalled load ends:** `vendorFetch` reads every body chunk by chunk and ticks (`readerTick`), Tesseract's logger ticks, and `withStall` fails the scripts' load and every `makeWorker` when nothing moved for `READER_STALL` 90 s (a `let`, the harness shortens it) — "the reader did not load within 90 s — no answer from github.io or the mirror" as "Reading failed: …" in the box, the auto card's row says the same instead of "Nothing could be read" (`failPending` takes the message), the error log has it, and the next reading tries again (`_ocrLoading` cleared); the dictionary loads outside the stall race, since it is optional and 2.5 MB. What it costs: a phone without the worker fetches the language file on every start and pays the 6 s probe once per session; a connection under about 20 kB/s on the language file is a stall now. Measured in the harness (no `sw.js`, the origin silent on every vendor file, a jsDelivr-like mirror with real content types): the reader reads 坚果 from the mirror 6.8 s after the call — the probe's one request to github.io, six files from the mirror, five of them in the cache for the worker —, a second worker fetches only the language file, a reload reads everything but the language file from the cache; a mirror silent on the language file ends the load at the stall text after 14 s with `READER_STALL` 8 s and the auto card's row names it; a worker that activates without claiming leaves the page uncontrolled (`controller` null, `reg.active` set) and the claim makes it controlled with the page's variables kept. Chromium's hard reload and its service-worker bypass both keep the controller, so the hard-reload case is not reproducible in the harness. Suite `verify-nosw` (those cases, the Diagnostics head line, the error log's vendor line); the english, twopass, vendor-mirror, vendor-hang, diag, reload, shell and savenow suites pass. Not yet field-checked — H's next photo without the VPN is the check: Diagnostics should say `SW yes` again after one start, or `SW no (registration …)` with the reader's files from the mirror and the reading ending either way. **The worker helps only while it controls the page:** H's diagnostics of the hanging reading said `SW no` with a desktop user agent (no "Mobile") — Chrome's "Desktop site" switch, ticked for the Bailian key page, was on for the app too, and a page without the worker fetches every vendor file straight from github.io. More → **Text recognition** shows how many of the ten reader files are on the phone and offers **Download** (`OCR_FILES`, `renderOcrRow`). The worker script itself only ever comes from github.io (it just carries the cache name). Still VPN-only: a first install if github.io is blocked, the 50 MB model download (jsDelivr caps files at 20 MB), the Claude provider. **VPN-off only: Qwen** (v173 — Bailian answered the phone without the VPN and refused through it).
- **Persistence:** IndexedDB `zeichentrainer` v3 — `progress` (keyPath `id`), `custom` (keyPath `id`, all cards; **the key is an id, not the text** — v118, H: the same character from another photo is a new card; `cardId(c)` = the text while it is free, else text + `#` + timestamp; cards and progress rows of v2 keep their text as the id, migrated in `onupgradeneeded`; every card is addressed by `id` — `cardOf`, the queue, `S.detail`, `S.editing`, `S.single`, `QSCARD`, `THUMB`, the list rows' `data-id` —, and `c` is only the text), `inbox` (keyPath `id`, photos), `settings` (keyPath `k`). No built-in deck; `deck()` is `S.custom`, sorted by creation time `at` (oldest first, so cards are learned in the order they were taken; the Cards list shows newest first). At boot, progress rows without a card are pruned.
- **Tabs:** Learn · Cards · Camera · More. The app opens on Learn. A tab tap always leaves the edit form; tapping **Cards** while a card is open returns to the list. **Rejected: a start screen** (v181–v182, H: "you either want to take a picture or use flash cards — a more user-friendly entry page" → two tiles, Learn and Take a photo, with a Home tab; then "two huge tiles, less text, more icon"; then H: "I'm not sure, maybe my idea was not good. Please find out if you find any good reference from other apps. Do not bring the start screen back unasked.) **Rejected: the Camera tab as the shutter** (v184, H: "if I tap the camera icon I should immediately be using the camera" — the tab rendered the inbox page and clicked the camera input in the same gesture; reverted in v186, H: "I don't like the current camera implementation (direct capture), please revert" — the tab opens the inbox page with Take photo and From album. A raised tint circle for the Camera tab was proposed with it and not asked for. The pick suite scrolls the Cards list to 900, taps Camera and checks the page is at 0, and again after a scroll inside the inbox; the english, cards, autocard and savenow suites pass. - **More in the learner's order (v257, H: "Polish. - **Every group of More ends in rounded corners, and the owner's inputs sit inside their rows (v282, H's screenshot of the Locked box: "warum sind die Ecken der weißen Boxen unten nicht rund?"):** two faults. The rounding rule for a group's last row was `.mrow:last-of-type`, and the section heads are divs like the rows, so only About's row — the page's last — ever got its bottom corners; every other group ended square (since More has had section heads). Now `.mrow:has(+.listhead)` rounds the row before every head too. And the password field stood as a `.field` under the Locked row, outside the white surface; it sits inside the row since — the input with its placeholder "Password" and Unlock on one line under the sentence (`.inrow`, the Feedback box's field look on `--card2`), "Wrong password." under it —, and the owner's Mirror address the same way with Check now beside it (until v281 a `.field` under the Mirror row). **The row is one quiet line since v283** (H: "Remove the description for the locked area, just call it admin log in … not very prominent"): **one line** since v284 (H: "polish") — the small grey label "Admin log in" (13 px), a lower password field (36 px, 15 px) and **Log in** as plain tint text (`.del`, no pill) side by side (`.inrow.admin`); v283 had the label above a full-height field and a soft button; no title and no sentence; the unlocked row reads "Logged in as admin" with **Log out** (its sentence stays, the owner's English); the keys "Locked", "Unlock" and the sentence left `lang.js`, "Admin log in" and "Log in" came in six languages; the admin suite's ids are unchanged. Measured in the harness: the Storage, Usage sharing, Locked and Mirror rows carry the 12 px bottom radius, a middle row none; the rows checked by screenshot in light and dark; the admin, english, order, shareapp, guide, feedback, users and progress suites pass. - **How to use the app (v259, H's to-do "a user guide (Gebrauchsanleitung)" for his friends — described first: in-app under More, six sections, text only; "Go"):** More → **Help** → "How to use the app" with the sentence "Six short sections: photo, characters, learning, cards, language, what stays on the phone." and **Open** (`#guide-open`, the section sits under Share); `S.mode="guide"`, `renderGuide` draws one white card (`.guide`, a `section` per topic with a hairline between, h2 17 px, p 16 px at line height 1.45) with ← Back and the badge "How to use the app"; the More tab stays lit, a tab tap leaves it, Back returns to More. The six sections (`GUIDE()`, twelve paragraphs since v357, every text through `t()` in six languages): Take a photo (the frame the app draws, corners, the handle, the reading by itself, the tap outside the frame; Save now), Fix the characters (the strip's buttons, other readings, drawing, the line input, Select; pinyin and meaning follow, the AI check, the flag), Learn (due cards then up to eight new, the two taps and the speaker; the four grades, Pull the next cards forward), Cards (newest first, search, filters, the detail's Test/Edit/Delete, + New with a drawn character; tags and the Learn filter), Language and meanings (the switch, the AI's meaning in that language, Translate all cards, the pill), What stays on the phone (cards and photos only here — export now and then; what the AI check sends; the daily counts and the Privacy switch; Feedback). **The rule:** the guide describes what the app does today, nothing planned; a screen that changes takes the guide's sentence with it in the same PR. No screenshots in it (they would go stale). Alternative rejected in the description: a page beside `privacy.html` — VPN for a friend in China, outside the language switch, nothing without a network. Suite `verify-guide` (the row after Share, Open, the six headings and eleven paragraphs, no "OCR" and no "·" in the text, the lit More tab, Back, a tab tap, the German headings and a German sentence); the guide checked by screenshot in English, German dark and Japanese; the english, admin, shareapp, feedback and order suites pass.
- **Three from the improvement list (v266, H: "Go 3, 4, 5"):** **the text goes to DeepSeek when it can** (`textProvider()`: a picture-capable active provider — Qwen on H's phone, slow on text since v208 — hands the text check, the picker's candidates and Translate all to DeepSeek when a DeepSeek key or the relay is there; DeepSeek itself or a provider without pictures stays; the picture keeps `pictureProvider()`; `aiAsk` and `aiCharAlternatives` take key, model and base from it, the AI row's line says "On: DeepSeek (deepseek-chat) checks the text, Qwen (qwen3.7-plus) reads the framed area …"; the providers and picture suites expect it) · **the notices as the app's own sheet** (`noteSheet(title,text)` = `askSheet` with `cancel:false` and OK alone, the backdrop and Escape close it: the clipboard fallbacks, No flagged cards, the export and import errors, and the import's sentence under the title "Import" — no `alert(` is left; the ask suite expects no browser dialog at all and checks the notice; screenshot in light and dark) · **the empty deck points to the guide** ("New here? The deletion itself is real from the first moment — a reload after five seconds shows the card gone. Photos → Delete N, the import and Reset keep their sheet. Suite `verify-ask` (no sheet, the line's text, button and role above the tab bar, card and progress gone from IndexedDB, Undo bringing both back at the old index, two deletions joined and both restored, the line gone after 5.4 s with the card still gone across a reload, the study-back deletion continuing the session and Undo showing the card again, the photo's deletion with the card's copy made and Undo dropping it again); the line checked by screenshot in light, dark, German and Japanese; the cards, edit, english, detail, photostore and export suites pass. Nothing is written to the phone (hard constraint 6); without a share sheet the notice "Sharing is not available here." shows. Suite `verify-share` (the button's place beside Delete under Edit, light and dark; a word card's PNG at 1080 px with its title and text, a two-line sign card's taller image with the wrapped meaning, a traditional card's text, a card without a picture shorter, the waiting card without Share, the notice without a share sheet, the German label); the four images and the row in light, dark and German checked by screenshot; the detail, cards and english suites pass. Field-confirmed 2026-09-06 (H: "Both look fine"); whether WeChat keeps the text beside the image H has not said.
- **Dropped: a card's history (idea 4 of the improvement list, 2026-09-06, H: "Why would i want the history of a card?" → "Drop it"):** a screen with the card's creation, every review with its grade and the AI's answer would answer a question the app already answers — a card that keeps failing is flagged after four fails in a row, and the detail shows interval, ease, review count and the next date. Not built; do not bring it back unasked.

More → All users shows a head row "with errors in the last 7 days N", in each phone's block "errors 2, last 2026-09-06 reading", and a section **"Errors on phones (N)"** at the end with every phone's messages ("Phone <id>, app version 271", then "2026-09-06 12:30 [reading] Reading failed: still at: loading the reader …" per line — these lines may wrap, like the device line). The Usage sharing sentence says "…, AI checks, and the app's error messages.", the guide's last paragraph "anonymous usage counts and the app's error messages", both in six languages; `privacy.html` names the messages and the removed characters (updated 6 September 2026). What it cannot do: a hang without an error (the silent reader without the VPN) leaves no trace — Diagnostics from the phone still tell those; a phone with the switch off sends nothing. Suite `verify-users` (the head row, the block line, the section with two phones' messages in order, the width rule exempting the message lines, `reportData().errors` after a logged error with its Chinese character stripped); the report's section checked by screenshot in light and dark, the Usage sharing row in German; the wechat, english, admin and guide suites pass; the static key check reports no missing key. (`progressData`, `progressHTML`; the tiles' numbers from `learnStats` and the progress rows). **Reviews per day** are counted since v274 in setting `daily` `{day: count}`, the last 60 days, bumped by every grade beside the streak's days list (`dailyBump`); nothing leaves the phone. The owner-ish counts (app opens, AI checks, the reader's and the models' work) left the row and live in the daily usage row and the owner's All users report (until v276 also in the shared text report, gone since v277). **Share report sends one image of the dashboard since v277** (v276 had put the dashboard's figures first in the text report — H: "Und beim Sharen bitte genauso" —, then: "You're still sharing too much! Only this please, sexy! The rest goes to the user reports"): `progressImage` draws the tiles, the 30-day strip, the deck bar with its legend and the week ahead on a canvas at 1080 px in the light look, like the shared card, with "识字 Zeichentrainer" and the day at the foot, and `shareProgress` hands the PNG `zeichentrainer-progress.png` to the share sheet with the title and "Day streak N, Cards learned N" as the text; without a share sheet the notice shows. The progress suite checks the PNG's name, type, title, text and width and saves it for a look; the admin suite expects the PNG in place of the text file; the picture suite no longer reads the report. Ten labels in six languages ("Day streak", "Cards learned", "Due today", "Reviews this week", "Last 30 days", "Still learning", "Known", "Coming up: …"; "New" was there). The Learning section sits at the top of More since v275 (H: "das gehört ganz nach oben, an erste Stelle"), Share second; the shareapp and guide suites read the sections' order accordingly, and the card-order suite waits for the chip's re-render and for the setting's write before its reload — both had raced the heavier More render. Suite `verify-progress` (a seeded deck of 3 known, 2 learning and 20 new cards with five review days: the four tiles, 30 blocks with the shades in order, the bar's three flex values and the legend, the two lines, Share report kept, no opens or AI counts in the row, a grade counting the day in `daily` and the second grade too, the count across a reload, the German tiles, legend and line); the row checked by screenshot in light, dark and German; the admin, english, order and guide suites pass, the static key check reports no missing key. - **The app's language (v253, H's to-do "Multi language UI and translations" — "We don't need 中文, because 中文 is already the original of the characters. We need German, English, French and other very important languages that most commonly need to be translated from Chinese"; described first with six languages and the meanings from the AI, built on "Go, with the button"):** English, German, French, Spanish, Japanese and Korean. **How it works:** `lang.js` (loaded before `app.js`, in the shell list of `sw.js`) holds `LANGS`, `LANG_LOCALE`, `L10N` — one column per language, **English is the key**: `t("Save card")` returns the translation or the English text itself, so a missing string shows English, never a key; `{0}`, `{1}` are filled by `t()`; `nOf(n,"card")` takes the count words from the table too, and a translation may carry the number as `{0}` where the language puts it elsewhere ("カード{0}枚", "{0}-mal"). `LANG` is set at boot from setting `lang`, else from the phone's language (`langDefault`, the first two letters of `navigator.language`), before the first render; `applyLangStatic` writes the header's capsules and the tab labels, which sit in `index.html`, and `document.documentElement.lang`; everything else asks `t()` while rendering. More → **Language**: its own section under Learning with the sentence "The app's own texts. Cards keep their Chinese, pinyin and meaning." and a chip per language in its own name (English, Deutsch, Français, Español, 日本語, 한국어); a tap switches at once (`setLang`) and keeps the choice. **Done in v253 (the first of five PRs):** the groundwork, the header and tabs, Learn (the empty deck, the done screen, the front's pill and hints, the back's pinyin, meaning, parts row, linked row, flag box, AI box, the grades with their intervals, the study actions), Cards (the search, + New, the AI bar, the chips, the rows' pills and status, the empty texts), the card detail (the badge, the hints, the actions, the SRS line with the date in the language's locale, the Delete sheet) and the sheet's Cancel; the leech flag's note is written in the current language. The suites run in English (the harness's locale) and pass; German and Japanese checked by screenshot on the front, the back, the list, the detail, the sheet and the Language row; the detection (a German and a Japanese browser locale open in that language), the switch and the setting across a reload measured in the harness. **Done in v254 (PR 2):** the Camera tab — the lead line, Take photo and From album, the inbox's head and rows with their date in the language's locale, Crop, Delete and Cancel, the frame's hints (the draw-a-frame sentence, the proposal's and the frame search's bars, the frame's title), the preview with its sentences, Read now and Image only, the reading bar with Save now, the saved lines, the failure sentences (`failText`: "Reading failed: {0}" and "Frame too small" in the language, while the step texts under "still at" and in Diagnostics stay English), the editor (Characters, Pinyin, Meaning, the flag with its note, Tags with its placeholder — shared with the forms —, Save card, Ask AI, Cancel, the head lines, the source lines under the meaning through "Meaning {0}, unverified", the picture mark's title, `aiErrText`), the strips' hints, the Select row, the script switch, the picker (its head with "Replace {0} with:" / "Add a character {0}:", Remove, Not here? Draw it, Ask AI, + before / + after, the AI's answers), the drawing sheet (both heads, Undo, Clear, Done, the status lines) and the notes written onto a card (the Save-now flags, the failed re-crop, "the reading looks wrong"); the header's Done capsule has its own key `capsule:Done` with its English in an `en` column, since Japanese and Spanish want another word on the capsule than on the Done buttons (`t()` falls back to `en`, then to the key). Checked by screenshot in German and Japanese: the inbox, the proposed frame with its preview, the editor after a reading, the picker, the drawing sheet, the saved note. **Done in v255 (PR 3):** More for everyone — Share the app and Feedback with their statuses, Your data (Export with the backup note and the photos checkbox, Import, Flagged cards), Online AI review (the state line in its four forms, the What-is-sent sentence, the checkbox, the Review queue's sentences and button), Storage, Learning with the Card order chips (`LEARN_ORDERS` labels through `t(l)`), On this phone (Progress with `workLines` — "on-device reader" and the count words reading/check translated —, Usage sharing with its sentence, state and id, Photos with its Delete button and sheet), the Advanced settings' locked row with Password, Unlock and "Wrong password.", About's sentence (`aboutText`); the Add form (every label and button, the placeholders, the errors, the autofill hint, "“{0}” added."); the Edit form (← Back, the Edit badge, the image field with Crop again and Remove image, the labels, the context word field, the flag, the AI button, Save changes / Cancel, Delete card, its sheet, the errors, the AI error line); the notices (the clipboard fallbacks, the export and import alerts, the import sheet and its sentence, the Reset sheet). Two local variables named `t` were renamed so they no longer shadow `t()` (`backupNote`'s `last`, the feedback handler's `tx`). Checked by screenshot in German and Japanese: More in light and dark, the Add form with its error, the Edit form and its Delete sheet; the static key check reports no missing key. **The tone (H, on seeing PR 3: "Be relaxed. This is a fun app. No formal, boring translations within the app. Customers are passionate language learners. Same for the design and layout."):** every language speaks to the learner as a friend — German in "du", French in "tu" (the whole column was switched from "vous" in v255, the imperatives with it), Spanish in "tú", Korean in the friendly 해요 style (every 합니다 ending switched in v255, "감사합니다" → "고마워요"), Japanese in the plain-polite です/ます that apps use — nothing stiff, no "Veuillez", no "Sie". **Done in v256 (PR 4): the meaning in the chosen language from the AI** — H chose the button over an automatic run, since it costs an AI call per batch of cards. Both prompts are functions now (`aiSystem()`, `picSystem()`) and ask for "natural German meaning … in German only" through `meaningLangName()` (`LANG_NAME` in `lang.js`, the languages' English names); every AI answer carries `ml` = the language asked for (`aiAsk`, `aiReadPicture`), and **a card stores the language of its meaning as `ml`** (absent = English — the dictionary, the phrasebook, the offline model and every card before v256; `mlOf`, `setMl`): the Read preview's card takes it from the AI's answer, a meaning typed by hand in the preview, the Add form or the Edit form counts as the app's language, the Edit form's AI button and the Add form's autofill carry the answer's, Accept on a suggestion takes the suggestion's, an untouched meaning keeps its language. **The meaning check by language** (`saneM`, in place of the v97 "no Latin letters" rule): an answer that is the Chinese text itself is dropped, an all-Han answer too — except in Japanese, where a meaning may be kanji alone (供給); kana and hangul count as a meaning. More → Language: the sentence reads "The app's own texts and the meaning of new cards. Cards keep their Chinese and pinyin." (v278, H: "Why mention the AI here?" — the meaning comes in the chosen language through the AI check, which every phone has through the relay; the rare English card carries its pill; until v277 the sentence said "and, with the AI, the meaning"), and under the chips a row **Meanings** — "N cards have their meaning in another language." with the button **"Translate all cards into Deutsch"** (the language's own name; `translateRowHTML`, `#translate-all`, shown only while such cards exist and an AI is set up, in English too: a phone switched back to English sees "Translate all cards into English" for its German cards): `translateAll` sends the cards whose `ml` differs from the app's language to the AI in batches of `TRANSLATE_BATCH` **5** (20 until v260 — H's screenshot of 99 Korean cards: "번역 중 0 / 99…" standing still, "no real-time progress": the count could move only after the first answer, and Qwen through the relay takes long for 20 cards; since v261 the row shows the moving bar with the count of the card the AI is on — `TRANSLATE.at`, set as each batch goes out — so the line moves every few seconds) (**the run's state lives in `TRANSLATE`, not in the row** — v257, H the same evening: "pressed Translate all, changed page, pressed again and no reaction": the tab change had re-rendered More, the loop wrote its progress into the old row and a guard swallowed the second tap; now `translateRowHTML` draws the row from the state, `translateRefresh` re-queries the row by id after every batch, a tap during a run shows the progress, the button is disabled meanwhile, the finished line stays until the language is switched (`setLang` clears a finished run), Learn or Cards re-render when the run ends, and a run stops at the next batch when the language was switched meanwhile) (`aiCardPayload` with `translate:true` says "translate the meaning into German (it is in English now); keep zh and p unless clearly wrong"), takes only the meaning and its language from each answer — the text and the pinyin stay —, **stages every answer instead of writing the card** (v264, H: "make translation consistent and make sure that cards are not mixed in different languages if the translation was interrupted"): setting `translateStage` `{lang, m:{id:{m,from}}}` — `from` = the meaning the card had when it was asked, so a meaning typed meanwhile is not overwritten; an answer without a meaning is staged as `skip` so the run can end — and **the cards change together in one IndexedDB transaction** (`idbPutMany`, `applyStage`) only when every card has its answer; until then Learn and Cards show the old meanings, all in one language, with the pill on every card, the row says "Translating 20 of 43 … The cards change together when all are done.", an interruption keeps the stage and the resumed run asks only for the cards not staged yet (`inStage`), a switched language drops the stage (`setLang`, and the follow-up run of v263), `running` stays set until the cards and the settings are written; then "Done — 43 cards translated." and the button goes; a failed call shows "The AI could not be reached. 20 translated, 23 left. It goes on by itself when the AI can be reached again." and keeps the button; offline it says so. **An interrupted run goes on by itself** (v262, H: "Please continue translating after being interrupted"): the run is remembered in setting `translateRun` `{lang, at}` from its start until no card is left (`rememberTranslate`), and `resumeTranslate` picks it up again 2.5 s after boot, on `online` and on foreground (`visibilitychange`) — when the app's language is still the run's, cards are left, an AI is on and the phone is online; **a language switched during or between runs retargets the translation** (v263, H: "If the language is changed during translation then all the cards have to be translated into the new language" — until v262 the switch dropped the run): a running loop stops at its next batch and `translateAll` starts a fresh run for the new language at once, a waiting run is re-remembered with the new language and resumed by `setLang`; each batch stores the language it was asked in (`lang` captured at the request, not `LANG` at the answer), so a batch answered after the switch carries the old language and the follow-up run translates those cards again. So a restart, a lost connection or Android cutting the background page's requests no longer ends the translation, and every card ends in the language the app shows; the row shows the progress when More is open. Suite `verify-translate` (a failed run keeps the setting and the line names the resume, a reconnect resumes and finishes it, a reload with the setting resumes at boot without a tap, a switch while a run waits resumes it in the new language, a switch mid-run — two German batches answered, then French ones — ends with every card French and the French Done line; staged: after two batches no card has changed and the stage holds ten, a cut connection leaves every card in the old language with the stage and the run remembered, the reconnect asks only for the remaining cards — the call count says so — and at the end every card changes together with the stage and the run gone). The parts row stays English (the dictionary's). A card filled without the AI shows an English meaning marked pending until the auto run's suggestion, which carries `ml`, is accepted. Suite `verify-translate` (no row in English on an English deck, the row and the button in German with the count, two batches for 26 cards with the German prompt and the translate ask, every meaning and `ml` changed and persisted, the Add form's autofill and a typed meaning, a suggestion accepted, the Edit form's typed meaning, `saneM` in German, Japanese and Korean, the way back to English dropping `ml`, a dead call's line); the Meanings row checked by screenshot in German, light and dark — the button sits under the sentence, as the Feedback row's Send, since its label is long in every language. The ai, addai, edit, busy, picture, savenow, recrop, aibad, aikept, aizh, admin and english suites pass (the aibad suite reads `aiSystem()` since). **Done in v258 (PR 5, H: "What is PR 5?" → described → "Go"): the pill for a meaning in another language** — a card whose `ml` differs from the app's language carries a grey pill with that language's own name ("English" on a German phone, "日本語"; `mlPill`, `langName`, `.pill.lang` in the plain pill look, title "The meaning is in another language than the app.") after the meaning on the back (Learn and the detail, `backHTML`) and on the pills line of the Cards list beside Traditional; it goes once the card is translated, by Translate all or an accepted AI check. Nothing else changes, no AI call. Suite `verify-translate` checks the list, the detail, the Learn back, the pill gone after a translation and a Japanese one; the back and the list checked by screenshot in German, light and dark; the english, cards, detail, link, trad and flag suites pass. The multi-language plan of v253 is complete with it. **One meaning per language on the card (v265, idea 2 of the improvement list, described first, "Go"):** `ms` = `{en:"…", de:"…"}` holds every meaning a card got, by language — written wherever a meaning gets its language (`setMl` records `card.m` under it) —, `m` stays the meaning shown and `ml` its language. `syncMeanings()` at boot and in `setLang` gives every card the meaning it already has in the app's language, in one transaction (`idbPutMany`), so a switch back to a known language is instant and costs no AI call; a card without one keeps its meaning and the pill, and Translate all fills it. A typed meaning replaces only its own language (the others stay); a changed Chinese text drops the other languages' meanings (`applyCardUpdate`), since they described the old text; the offline model's meaning is recorded under `en`; the Cards search finds every language's meaning. Cards from before v265 get `ms` at the first start. Export and import carry `ms` like any field; an older app ignores it. Suite `verify-translate` (the switch back to English instant with no call, the three cards made in German keeping it with the pill and the row counting them, German back from `ms` on the next switch, a typed meaning replacing one language, a changed text dropping the others, the migration at boot); the english, cards, edit, addai, ai, export, admin, detail and link suites pass. A static check in the harness lists every `t()` key and count word of `app.js` that a column lacks (`node -e` over `lang.js`); at v254 the columns lack only the count words of the More screen and the owner's reports. The owner's tools stay English: Diagnostics, All users, the owner's Feedback row, the usage report, the Unlocked row, the AI setup form, Mirror, Text recognition, Offline translation, Reset, `privacy.html` (their count words go through `nOf` and would follow a switched language — H uses English). Japanese and Korean are mine and unchecked by a native speaker; not yet field-checked on the phone.

### The flow (AI-first — "nobody wants to see OCR if not necessary")
1. **Camera:** `<input type="file" capture="environment">` (Take photo) or `<input type="file" multiple>` (**From album** — several at once, all land in the inbox, the first opens in crop mode; `addPhoto` per file) — or **a screenshot shared to the app from another app** (v163, H: "a fast and easy function to translate screenshots": the manifest's `share_target` posts the files to `./share`, the worker's `shareIn` parks them in the cache `zt-share` and answers with a redirect to `./?share=1`, the page's `takeShared` moves them into the inbox through `importPhotos` — the same path as the album, the first in crop mode; the installed app only, a browser tab has no share target; Chrome picks the new manifest up on a later launch, so the entry in the share sheet may take a day or a re-install — not yet field-checked) (a "Done, no card" link beside Cancel that also deleted the photo, v163–v167, went in v168 — H: "What's the difference between Cancel and Done, no card?" … "Cancel only"; Cancel keeps the photo, the inbox's Delete removes it) → the photo is normalized (EXIF baked in, max 1600 px) and stored in `inbox`. A pulsing "Processing photo …" placeholder appears at once; the inbox re-renders on `visibilitychange`; pull-to-refresh is off (`overscroll-behavior-y: contain`).
**A photo becomes a card by itself (v325, H, 2026-09-08: "Develop concept where no frame is seen during the first scan at all. Just the magic wobbling over the image. And then the result is the finished card. But if I want to edit something and go to edit mode, then I get a frame which I can adjust." — described first, then "the result can be the finished card, plus an option to save and accept or to edit", then "It can be saved by itself. Is that recommended?" → yes, with the reasons; "Go"):** a photo that opens by itself — taken, the first from the album, shared — shows **no frame and no preview**. The photo stands full width with a soft band of light sweeping across it (`.shotwrap .scan`, the kind of the v288 sweep, which went because the frame then carried the dashes; a faint veil under reduced motion) — **the band moves by transform alone since v332** (H at v331: "Der Bildscanning-Effekt hakt oben links. Muss flüssig laufen"): until v331 the sweep animated `left`, a layout property the main thread draws, so it froze whenever the reader's copies and flood fills held the thread, and the skewed band's top corner popped in at the top left on every loop; now `translateX` from −150 % to 250 % of the band's width with `will-change:transform`, linear, so the compositor keeps it moving while the page works and the band starts and ends well outside the box. Measured in the harness: the transform changes between two frames while a synthetic 200 ms main-thread stall runs; not yet field-checked — the next photo is the check: the light gliding evenly while the app reads and the bar "Reading the text …", **Cancel** at the right of the row. The card exists from the first moment: `proposeFrame`'s hidden branch calls `saveNow(id,true)` — the placeholder with the proposal as `reading.rect`, `reading.auto`, and `AUTO[id]`, `PENDING[id]`, `QSCARD[id]` set **before the cut is made**, so the quick look's placement meanwhile goes to `PLACED` and no frame is ever drawn (a first cut read `CROP.followed` before the awaits and lost the placement). The reading runs as for a Save-now card — **the quick look and the close look place for a waiting card whose frame is the app's** (`READ_APP[id]&&!PLACED[id]`; until v324 they ran only while a crop frame stood, so a Save-now card never got the quick look's frame), the AI's box as before —, and `finishPending` fills it: the placed frame as `frame`, its cut as `img`, no note, and **the finished card in the photo's place** (`resultHTML`: the front's photo box and text box, the back with pinyin, the speaker, the meaning, the parts row and the linked row, the flag box when flagged; a tap on the photo box shows the whole picture and back) with **Edit** and **Delete card** in the row (`data-resedit`, `data-resdel`), the AI suggestion box under it when one comes. **The row is the card detail's own since v344** (H's screenshot of three finished cards: "Please polish, especially edit and delete", then "Looks a little squeezed?"): until v343 it was the photo row's `.meta` — the photo's timestamp in the monospace font at the left, a small soft Edit pill and "Delete card" as plain tint text two sizes larger, the three of them touching the parts row above; now the finished card carries `.detailacts`, the same two-column grid of equal 15 px buttons the detail's Edit / Flag / Share / Delete card sit in (`.btn` and `.btn.danger`), with the detail's 16 px above them and a little more room around the result (`.shot:has(.result)` 12/14 px). The photo's timestamp is gone from that row — it belongs to a photo, and a photo row keeps it beside Crop and Delete. Nothing else changes; Edit still opens the form with the frame, Delete card still acts at once with Undo. The autocard suite reads the buttons from `.shot .acts button, .shot .detailacts button` since; the english, savenow and recropai suites pass; checked by screenshot in light, dark and German. Not yet field-checked. **Flagged only when the reading is doubtful**: `mt.suspect`, a bad AI answer, or a weak reader score the AI check did not confirm — note "the reading looks unsure — check text, pinyin and meaning" (six languages); a Save-now card keeps its v245 flag. **Edit** opens the Edit form from the camera (`S.editFrom="camera"`, `S.editOpenFrame`) with Crop again already open — the photo with the frame the card was cut with, adjustable, no reading until it moves (v244) —; Back, Save changes and Delete return to the Camera tab and the result. **Delete card** acts at once with Undo (v268); the photo stays with Crop and Delete, Undo brings the result back. **Cancel** while it reads (`cancelAuto`) drops the placeholder (`dropAuto`: the card, the byPhoto count, the thumbnail) and keeps the photo with Crop. **Nothing readable, or a failed reading, makes no card** (`failPending`'s auto branch): the placeholder goes and the row says "Nothing could be read. Tap Crop to frame the text by hand." A restart while it reads: `resumePending` reads the card again and restores `AUTO` and `QSCARD`, so the result shows in the row when done. From album with several photos: the first only, the others wait with Crop. **What stays where it was:** Crop (the hand's frame with the preview, Save now and Save card), Crop again, the tap outside the frame and the marching dashes — in those views; the flow below describes them. **A photo of a user interface or of several separate labels, one card per element (v357–v358, H's rice cooker panel — eleven buttons, 低卡饭 柴火饭 快煮 粗粮饭 汤/粥, 时 分, 保温/取消 预约 开始 功能: "I want that if such a picture comes, you automatically detect the different words on it and make single flash cards for each of them"; described in three lines, "Go" — then, on v357's test: "No. It doesn't work like that. I think you have to find out if the image is a user interface and then put a card for each and every single element of it", and then: "Also for all kinds of signs and labels"):** measured on that photo first — the reader reads garbage from it (说, 队定还, 下昌。。功双, every pass at 0), so the picture goes to the AI as any weak reading does, and the answer already carries every label with its own pinyin, its own meaning (v300) and its own box (v312): eleven cards' worth of data in the one call the app already makes. What the pixels alone cannot do: a clustering of the photo's ink found 8 of the 11 labels and lost the whole left half to one brightness threshold, so the split follows the model, not geometry. **The model is asked one question — do these texts stand apart? — and lists them** (`picSystem()`'s `"apart"` and `"labels":[{zh,p,m,box},…]`, `aiReadPicture`'s `apart`/`labels`): true for a user interface (the control panel of an appliance, a remote, a keypad, a lift panel, a vending machine, a ticket machine, a cash machine, **a screenshot of a phone app**) **and for several signs, labels, buttons, menu items or packages standing next to each other** — a menu board, a shelf of price labels, a wall of notices, a building directory, a bus stop board, **a row of shopfronts**, the care instructions on a clothing label, **the section headings on a package** (配料表, 净含量, 保质期), **the field labels of a form or a receipt** (发票, 金额, 日期), **the rows of a timetable or a price list** (v359, H: "Brand names, signs, logos, user interfaces. What else can you think of?" → a list of candidates; v361, H: "implement all as suggested, but no Icons and logos with no text and no Menu prices") —, whose Chinese texts each name their own button, setting, item or thing, so that a learner would learn them one by one; false when the lines belong to one text (a poster's title and its credits, a sign's two lines, a brand above a product name, a label's name and its ingredients); **a price or an amount is never an element of its own, but it stays in the text of the item it belongs to** (v361, H: "no Menu prices" — a menu board gives one card per dish, not one for 38元; v363, H: "if they're part of the meaning then they must be included. Remember the 24H rule" — v336's number with its unit: 宫保鸡丁 38元 keeps its price as 24H存包 keeps its hours and 净含量380ml its millilitres, and only a price standing on its own gets no card). **A phone app's screen is one of them, and its labels are tagged App (v367, H's six screenshots of Meituan, Taobao and Xianyu — the home screen's icon grid, an account page, a tab bar: "Bitte nehme auch die funktionen der wichtigsten chinesischen Apps mit auf (screenshots). Die koennen wir speziell trainieren."):** `picSystem()`'s `apart` list names the cases — a super app's grid of function icons (外卖, 团购, 酒店民宿, 闪购), the rows of an account page (我的订单, 待付款, 待收货, 退款/售后), a tab bar (首页, 视频, 购物车, 我的), the tab strip over a list (关注, 推荐, 新发), the blocks of a wallet or an order page —, and **App** is the ninth kind of v364 (`KINDS`, "kind:App" in six columns: App, Appli, アプリ, 앱), so a screenshot's labels all carry the tag and the filter sheet studies them as a group. **A label's box has its own floor** (`LABEL_MIN` 0.005 beside `PIC_MIN` 0.02 in `picBox`/`picScale`, both taking it as an argument): the v293 rule that a box must cover a fiftieth of the picture is right for a poster's text block and wrong for one label of a 2520-pixel-tall screenshot — 外卖 is 30 pixels tall, 1.2 %, so every label was dropped and H's home screen made one card of all fifteen. Measured on H's own screenshot (1080×2520, Qwen's answer mocked with the grid's fifteen labels): **fifteen cards, each with its own text, pinyin and meaning, each frame on its own label** and each tagged App. What it cannot do: a small label in a tall screenshot is a few pixels tall in the 800-pixel picture the AI sees — an account page's fine print may come back wrong or not at all. Suite `verify-panel` (`app`: H's Meituan home screen with the fifteen labels — at least twelve cards, every one tagged App with its own picture and a frame under 6 % of the photo's height, the head line "15 cards from this photo"); the kind, english, picture, aiframe, autocard and guide suites pass; checked by screenshot. Not yet field-checked — H's next screenshot shared to the app is the check. **Rejected with it: a card for an icon or a logo with no text** (v359 described it in three lines — the picture the icon, the text the Chinese name of what it means, flagged because the model names it rather than reads it; H, v361: "no Icons and logos with no text"). Do not bring either back unasked. the labels come **one entry per element, all of them, in reading order**, the small label under a bigger one (长按童锁 under 洗衣液) included — on an interface nothing is fine print —, an element printed on two lines (加速 above 省时) as one entry with one rectangle, and an element that reads 汤/粥 with the slash kept in its own entry. The joined `"p"` and `"m"` cannot be split on `" / "` — `pySpaced("汤/粥")` is "tāng / zhōu" and its meaning "soup / congee", so two of H's eleven labels each add a part and the joined strings come back as 13 pinyin and 12 meanings for 11 lines, which is exactly the photo the feature exists for; each entry keeps its own. The labels are read from the raw answer, past the v312 fine-print filter, so a sub-label is not dropped on the way. **The app checks only what it can check better than the model** (in `cropSign`'s box branch, `SPLIT[id]`): `SPLIT_MIN` 2 to `SPLIT_MAX` 30 elements (H's washing machine carries 23, his dishwasher's lower panel only two — 加速省时 beside 轻载模式), every box read on the same scale (`picScale`: `picBox` reads numbers as fractions, as the picture's pixels or on the 0–1000 grid on its own, so a mixed answer would put some labels somewhere else entirely) — **the labels are read the way most of them are read (v379, H's washing machine at v378: "Waschmaschine hat nicht funktioniert", and the log named the gate — "the AI calls these 19 texts separate labels, but their boxes are not all on the same scale — one card"):** the model's grid put the panel's last column at x 770–830 of an 800 px picture, so that one box alone came out over the picture's width and `picBox` took it for the 0–1000 grid while the other eighteen were pixels. One column overshooting the edge by a little says nothing about the answer's scale, so the majority decides now (`picBox` takes a `force`, the tally is counted before the labels are built, ties go to the union box's own reading, and a box read on the majority's scale is clamped to the picture as any box is — clamped **before** the size test, since a box forced onto another scale may lie wholly outside the picture and what is left of it must still be a box). It works both ways: an answer that is really on the 0–1000 grid with one small box that happens to fit under the picture's size reads that one as the grid too. A box that is nonsense on every scale is dropped and the other labels keep their cards (v360's rule), where until v378 the whole panel fell back to one card. Diagnostics says "18 px, 1 grid — the labels are all read as px" when they differed, no box covering the whole picture, the automatic-card path only (`PENDING[id]&&!RECROP[id]&&READ_APP[id]` — a frame drawn by hand still makes one card), an upright frame (a tilted panel keeps one card) and not the v340 pixel reading of the box (`altWon`: `boxes` are never rescaled with it). **What v357 asked and v358 does not:** whether two boxes stood side by side in a row band and whether any box was taller than twice its width — H's correction: a panel whose buttons sit in one column would have failed both, and the geometry was the app's invention, not a judgement the pixels can make. The price: a poster whose model wrongly answers `ui:true` is split into its lines; the question decides, and the model is the one that can see the picture. Each box is then snapped on its own (`snapBox` with n=1 and that label's character count, its `beyond` bands ignored — a neighbouring label is not this label's cut-off line), gets `FRAME_ROOM` and is mapped onto the photo by `photoFrameOf` — `frameOnText`'s own upright mapping without its side effects, with the minimum measured in the copy's pixels (`SPLIT_PX` 16) and the frame grown around its centre to `CROP_MIN` 8.5 layer pixels, since a button 35 px tall in a 1600 px photo is under 8 px on a 338 px layer and is a good card picture all the same. **`splitCards` in `finishPending`** builds each element with the existing builder — a one-line copy of the reading (`res`, `mean`, `full`, `nmt`, `cardImg` cleared, `ai` narrowed to that label) through `readingCard`, so every card gets its parts row, its meaning language and its metadata exactly as a card of its own would — the placeholder becomes the first element's card, the rest are saved beside it with their own 16:9 window (v329) and their own `frame`, so Crop again starts on that label; ids are made unique against the deck and each other (`cardId` appends `Date.now()`, identical inside one loop), and **all the cards are written in one transaction** (`idbPutMany`, v264's rule — half of them saved while the placeholder still carries its `reading` would be read again at the next start and doubled). The photo's row lists them (`QSMORE[id]`, the head line "11 cards from this photo" in six languages, each card with the detail's Edit and Delete card, a hairline between); Delete removes one and the v268 Undo line brings it back. Anything that does not pass the check makes one card as before, with a Diagnostics line saying why. **Every test that keeps one card says so, and a straightened panel splits too (v374, H's washing machine at v373 — the rice cooker's cards were right and the washer made one card of all 24 labels: "Aber die waschmachine hat er immer noch nicht geschafft"; the diagnostics arrived after a reload and ended at “Asking the AI about the picture …”):** two faults, both about not being able to see. **(1) The reading's later lines lived in memory.** `readingStatus` saves the log 0.8 s after each step (v267), but the frame's, the snap's and the split's own lines were pushed straight into `READLOG` — so a reload (an update, the v373 brightening pass, the app reopened) left a diagnostics text that stops at the AI's question and never says what happened after it. Every one of those 25 places goes through **`logRead(text)`** since, which pushes, trims and saves like `readingStatus`. **(2) Half the split's tests were silent.** `pic.labels` were only split on the automatic-card path, with the app's own frame, no pixel reading of the box, an upright base and a straightening under 1.5° — and when one of those failed, nothing was logged at all; the same for the last gate in `finishPending` (the frames cut, but the picture answer not used as the reading). Both say their reason now (“the AI calls these 24 texts separate labels, but the frame was turned by hand — one card”, “the labels have their frames, but the picture answer was not used as the reading — one card”), and `aiReadPicture` logs the answer's shape as it comes: “the AI's answer: 24 lines, apart yes, 24 labels (24 usable), 0 boxes, meaning 241 characters”. **And the tilt test is gone:** `photoFrameOf` maps a label's rectangle back through the straightening angle exactly as it maps the union frame, so a panel the reader straightened splits like any other — the tilt costs each label the little room its bounding box adds, not its card. A frame the **hand** turned still keeps one card (that angle is the user's), and so does a re-crop, a hand-drawn frame and the v340 pixel reading. Measured in the harness on H's washing machine with 23 labels: at 0° 23 cards as before, and with the straightening forced to 4° and 9° **23 cards** where v373 made **one, silently** (verified by running the same probe on the stashed v373 code). What this does not settle: H's own washer reading was not straightened (its log carries no “straightened by” line), so the tilt is not what stopped it — the cause is still unknown, and the next attempt's log is what will say. Suite `verify-panel` (the washer at 4°: 23 cards and the split's line in the log); the autocard, diag and aiframe suites pass. Not yet field-checked — H's washing machine taken again is the check, and its Diagnostics now name the gate that stopped the split. **The labels are placed row by row, in the order the model read them (v376, H's washing machine at v375 — the split worked and every card showed a neighbour's label: "beim 2. Anlauf hat es geklappt, aber leider fehlerhaft"):** the answer's boxes for that panel are an evenly spaced grid — 130, 200, 270, 340 across and 255, 300, 345 down, every box 50×35 — a plausible layout rather than a measurement, and the panel's own rows are not evenly spaced (an icon row stands between the first and the second), so the model's third row sat a full row above the labels it named. v359's per-label search weighs what a run looks like far above where it sits, so each anchor walked to whatever ink was nearest: five pairs of labels took the same characters (快速 = 袜子, 大件 = 内裤, 羊毛 = 智洗烘 …) and six found nothing at all. What the model does get right is the **reading order and the topology** — which label stands in which row, and in which column of it —, so that is what is used now (`labelPlan`): every label's own scan nominates one run per row of ink near it (`labelScan`, v359's machinery with its local cut, looking `LB_FAR` 4 box heights up and down instead of `LB_UP` 1.6, and letting a row be `LB_TALL` 2.2 box heights tall, since a panel's row of labels carries its fine print); the nominations of all the labels are clustered into the picture's own rows (`labelClusters`); the answer's boxes are grouped into rows (`labelRows`); the rows are laid onto the clusters **in order** by a small dynamic program, so a cluster may be skipped — the icon rows are; and inside a cluster the row's labels are laid onto its runs in order (`alignRow`, again a dynamic program: each run used once, a label that finds none costs `LB_MISS`), so **no two labels can take the same run**. Then `labelRect` runs again, **pinned** to that row and that run — the region is the run with a little room, the row nearest it wins outright, and a row another label was placed on is never taken as this label's second line, while a spare row of ink standing under `LB_PAR` two or more of a row's labels is a row of its own (a panel's icons) and not their second line either, so 保温 above 取消 still joins. A label the plan could not give a run of its own keeps the answer's box but is held to its row. Nothing lines up — fewer clusters than rows, or under `LB_KEEP` half the labels placed — and the plan is dropped: every label is placed on its own as before, and the log says so. Measured on H's own panel photo with a Qwen-style grid: **19 of 19 cards on their own label and none on a neighbour's**, where the same photo and grid gave 10 of 19 before. Diagnostics: "the 19 labels stand in 3 rows, and 18 of them found their own place in the picture's own rows of characters". What it costs: one extra scan per label before the plan, a few tens of milliseconds on a panel. Suite `verify-panel` (`edgeScale`: the eleven rice-cooker labels with the last box's right edge ten pixels past the picture — eleven cards and the tally line, where v378 kept one; `mixedScale`: one box nonsense on every scale, dropped, the other three keeping their cards; H's washing-machine photo with the drifting grid: at least 17 cards, at least 15 of them on their own label with no card holding more than a third of a neighbour's, and the plan's line in the log); the rice cooker's eleven frames are unchanged, the dishwasher's 上层 (38–58 → 48–56 %), the oven's 功能 (16–37 → 21–34 %) and 轻载模式 (63–80 → 65–73 %) come out tighter, the Meituan screen is unchanged to the percent; the aiframe, autocard, picture, badpic, savenow and english suites pass. Not yet field-checked — H's washing machine taken again is the check: a card per button, each showing its own label. **The AI's label boxes are a drawing, not a measurement, and then the cards get the frame's own picture (v380, H's washing machine at v379 — 20 cards with the right texts and every picture its neighbour's button: "Immernoch falsch, aber anders."):** the answer's grid stood a cell and more to the right of the panel — 混合's box on 快速, 单脱水's on 煮洗, 煮洗's on the dial —, and the placement of v376 followed it, so every card showed the next label. Rebuilt in the harness on H's own panel photo with a grid drifted the same way: **nothing in the picture can tell the two readings apart.** A drifted grid is internally consistent, and a button panel's cells are as evenly spaced as the model's boxes, so a shift by one cell fits the ink exactly as well as the truth: the correct assignment's overlap with the boxes is zero for every label, the plan places as many labels either way (14 against 17, and 16 at a full cell's drift), the run widths say nothing (a two- and a three-character label make runs of the same width on a panel of equal buttons), the gaps fit either way because the dial's own run stands where the drifted grid wants it, and an affine fit absorbs a shift in its intercept. Every signal the pixels offer points at the neighbour. What gives it away is the answer itself: **twenty labels of two, three and seven characters all got a box of exactly 60 × 40 pixels.** A measurement follows the characters; a drawing does not. So `templateBoxes(lab,W,Hh)` asks whether at least four fifths of the label boxes are of one size (within `LB_TMPL` a tenth of the median in width and height, so the box or two the picture's edge clips does not count) while the character counts of those labels differ — and when they are, the boxes are not used to cut a picture at all: every card of that photo keeps its own text, pinyin, meaning and tag and gets **the frame's own picture** (`PLACED[id]`, else the frame the AI saw), the same picture for all of them. Diagnostics says "the AI's 19 label boxes are all the same size — a drawing of the grid, not a measurement: every card gets the whole picture" and "the AI calls these 19 texts separate labels — one card each, all with the frame's own picture". An answer whose boxes vary with the labels — H's rice cooker, his Meituan home screen, every answer that has worked so far — is a measurement and is placed as before by v359–v376. What it costs: a panel whose grid is drawn *and* happens to be centred loses the per-label cuts it would have got, twenty cards of one photo carry the same picture, and that picture is stored once per card. Why nothing cleverer: a card showing the neighbour's button teaches the wrong thing, and once the boxes are a drawing there is no measurement left to be clever with. Tried and dropped on the way, all unmerged: a run's width against the label's character count in the row's own units (the runs of a panel are all a button wide, whatever the label says), every run of a band nominated instead of the one nearest the label (the cluster then holds the row's true runs and the drifted boxes still choose the neighbours), and the plan's own placed count as a warning sign (14–17 of 19 whether the grid is true or a cell off). Measured in the harness on H's panel photo with the drifted grid: 19 cards, each with its own text, all with the panel's picture, none holding a neighbour's label — where the same photo and grid gave twelve cards on their neighbour before. Suite `verify-panel` (the wash case rewritten: the drifted grid, the two log lines, 19 cards, one frame for all of them, every card with a picture; the rice cooker, the Meituan screen, the column, the dishwasher, the oven, both scale cases, the 23-label panel and the cut-off answer unchanged); the aiframe, autocard, picture, badpic, english and kind suites pass. Not yet field-checked — H's washing machine taken again is the check: twenty cards with the right texts, each showing the panel. **A Latin line cost the whole answer, and a box's height is not the tell (v381, H's washing machine at v380 — one card with all nineteen labels in its text: "Immernoch falsch", and the log's last line "the labels have their frames, but the picture answer was not used as the reading — one card"):** two faults, both reproduced in the harness from H's own answer. **(1) Qwen answered a "Bra" line** among the nineteen Chinese ones — a Latin-only line, which the prompt asks it to leave out —, so the reading's lines held twenty while every later comparison counts the Chinese lines only: `signPreview`'s guard "an AI check applies as long as the text was not edited afterwards" measured 19 filtered lines against the answer's 20 and **deleted `sg.ai`**, `renderShots` ran that guard between the picture branch's `SIGN[id]` and `finishPending`, and the split's last gate then found no picture answer — the card was made from the reading with its word-by-word gloss, flagged, and DeepSeek was asked about it afterwards. `aiReadPicture` drops a line without a Chinese character before anything else now — with its pinyin, its meaning and its line box, so the rest stays aligned — and says so ("a line of Latin letters left out of the AI's answer: Bra"); a line of digits and a unit alone holds no Chinese either and goes the same way, since the guard would otherwise throw the whole answer away for it. **(2) The template test of v380 read the boxes' heights:** H's grid gives every box the same width (60 px) and its row's own height (50 px in the top row, 30 px in the two below), so four fifths of them were never "of one size" and the rule never fired. The width alone decides now — a label of k characters is k characters wide, while a box's height is its row's style. Measured in the harness on H's own answer over his panel photo: 19 cards, each with its own text, all with the frame's picture, where v380 made one card of all of them. Suite `verify-panel` (the wash case carries H's two row heights and his Latin line since: the Latin line's log line, no Bra card, the drawing's two lines, 19 cards, one frame for all of them, every card with a picture); the aiframe, picture, badpic, autocard, english, kind and units suites pass. Not yet field-checked — H's washing machine taken again is the check: nineteen cards with the right texts, each showing the panel. **A drawn grid is asked again, one strip per row (v382, H at v381 — twenty cards with the right texts and the whole panel as every picture: "Die Bild crops sind noch falsch"; the option was described in three lines, "Go"):** the whole-panel picture of v380 is honest and useless, so before giving up on the boxes the app asks the model once more. Measured first, on H's own picture rebuilt at 800 × 715 from his screenshot: **the pixels cannot be segmented into per-label runs by any global scan** — one row profile over the whole width gives a single band for the label area (the dial glows through every row), the left and right blocks scanned apart merge rows 1 and 2 into one 120 px band, and a column profile per text row gives the left block's four labels as **one run**, because that half of the panel is dimmer. The local cut of v359 does work, but it needs an anchor that is roughly right. **The model is the lever:** H's rice cooker answered with measured boxes for eleven labels and its crops were right; his washing machine answered a lattice for twenty. So `stripBoxes` cuts the picture into one strip per row of the answer's own rows (at most `STRIP_MAX` three, the rows merged into three when there are more), each strip the full width with `STRIP_PAD` half the rows' pitch of room above and below — the drawn grid's y drifts as its x does —, and asks `aiReadPicture` again for each; every box that comes back is matched to a label by its text and mapped onto the picture, a label the strips do not name keeps the box it had. **Then the second answer is judged by whether the boxes moved** (v383, below): the median distance a box travelled against the median box width, and under `STRIP_MOVE` a quarter of a box the strips have measured nothing and the cards keep the whole picture as in v380; past it every label is placed by v359–v376 as any measured answer is. Diagnostics: "the AI is asked again, one strip per row", then "the strips measured 19 of the 19 labels, 141 % of a box away from the grid — every card gets its own picture" or "the strips answered with 19 boxes, but put them where the grid already had them — every card gets the whole picture". What it costs: three more Qwen calls and 10–20 s on a photo whose boxes are drawn, and nothing at all on any other answer.
